                                                              √
The Bridge Series C residual: real-quadratic structure over Q( 35),
        a Baker obstruction, and an explicit vector-valued
                      quantum modular law
                                             Arvin Hampton
                                                 539 Labs

                    Consolidated write-up, 19 October 2026 — revision 8
                              (internal working paper; not externally refereed)



                                                 Abstract
        Let Q7 (n, k) = n 2
                              + 7nk + 72 k(k + 1),     an indefinite binary quadratic polynomial whose
   quadratic part has discriminant 35. We study the orthant series Ps = n,k≥0 q Q7 (n,k) , which
                                                                                  P
   is the common non-theta remainder of the “Bridge Series” C = Ps − Hs and of Cthird =
              n2 +7nk+ 72 k(k−1)
   P
      n,k≥1 q                    , where Hs is a signature-(1, 1) Zwegers-type indefinite theta series. We
   prove the elementary decomposition            2Ps = E + 1 + θ3 − Θhol , in which Θhol is a Zwegers
   holomorphic part and E(q) = a∈K q (Na+7)/20 is the partial theta series of a single narrow ray
                                        P
                  √
   class K of Q(√ 35) modulo √      p2 p5 ∞1 ∞2 . The radial asymptotics at q → 1 have leading coefficient
   I = log(6 + 35)/(2 35); combined with Baker’s theorem this excludes every representation
   of C or Cthird as an algebraic combination of classical modular, mock (signature (1, 1)), unary,
   partial or false theta objects plus an o(1/t) error, and it excludes all quotient laws C · g = F
   and all scalar W7 slash laws. In        √ the opposite direction, the Siegel theta lift of K over the
   closed geodesic of the unit 6 + 35 yields a 140-label family of weight-0 Maass forms with
   eigenvalue 1/4 (129 of the labels give nonzero functions), whose labels carry a 72-dimensional
   representation of PSL2 (Z) (the lattice is odd, so a characteristic vector enters the T -law); the
   lift functions themselves span a 42-dimensional S, T -invariant subspace. The order-t0 S-cocycle
   of the fundamental-domain cone sums is the Lewis–Zagier period function of these forms; it
   is real-analytic on R \ {0} for the automorphy factor |cx + d|. The 42-dimensional function
   span splits S, T -equivariantly as 36 Eisenstein plus 6 cuspidal dimensions; the cusp part is
   spanned by 6 fixed linear combinations of 12 explicit dihedral Maass forms (of level 19600
   by standard theory). (Revision 3 corrects the earlier “60 + 12”.) The leading-order (1/t)
   radial profiles obey the same S/T law with the factor 1/|x|; revision 3 proves this profile law
   in general by Poisson summation. Revision 4 adds an exact tensor factorisation of the label
   representation through SL2 (Z/140), and through SL2 (Z/4s(s − 2)) for the whole family Qs with
   s odd; it identifies the function span with the two matched local-parity sectors. It also adds
   exact dimensions of the function span, its Eisenstein part and its cusp part for s = 5, . . . , 51
   and s = 72, closed forms in s for the radial coefficients of Hs through t5/2 , and a single-kernel,
   two-chain construction of both parts of Cthird . For the scalar row we prove (given three earlier
   inputs) an exact twisted Herglotz/digamma closed form for the cocycle and a closed Gauss-
   sum formula for the dual cone sum ER . The cusp rows at rationals are shown numerically to
   be finite combinations of Hecke L-values at s = 0. Every statement is labelled Category A
   (proved, or exact, including exact finite computation) or Category B (floating-point or PSLQ
   evidence only, identification by citation only, interpretation, or conjecture); revision 4 applies
   this convention uniformly. Revision 5 (§13) proves the two-dimensional Euler–Maclaurin input
   F1, makes the E-part cocycle (with the 140-label vector law) Category A, proves the uL coset
   property for all s and the squarefree criterion h = 2ω(s) , and proves dim V = 2406 at s = 98.


                                                     1
      Revision 7 (§15) proves that the extra h-relations number exactly k(k − 1) at m = 2k 2 − 1
      (Proposition K1), derives 7s − 32 = −2Q(c2 ) from the lattice, shows that only the factor s − 2
      of ∆ is intrinsic while 7s − 32 is tied to the locked cone edge c2 = (−4, 1), gives exact closed
      forms through t13/2 for the parallel axes-cone object Hsaxes with a wall-independent universal
      piece, and adds t15/2 , t17/2 and the even-N Gauss data. Revision 8 (§§16–17, Appendix G)
      proves that the 140-label representation is isomorphic to the Weil representation of the even
      lattice N2 = {x ∈ Z2 : x2 even} (explicit intertwiner, exact in Z[ζ280 ]), hence to the dual Weil
      representation of T = ⟨−2⟩ ⊕ ⟨70⟩ ⊕ U ⊕ E8 (−1)2 , the transcendental lattice of an N2 -polarised
      K3 surface; it records, conditional on cited theorems, the K3 surfaces and their automorphisms,
      and states the implications for the Residual Ternary Algebra, fifth-order unification, even multi-
      k and K3, with corrections. No claim is made concerning HQCC termination, the twin prime
      conjecture, or the Riemann hypothesis.

1     Introduction
1.1     Origin and scope
The series studied here arise as the “Bridge Series C” in the S2 -11DM2 ET-X framework (repository
ArvinHampton/539-Labs-Master, directory docs/S2-11DM2ET-X-Axiomatic-Book/, file Resi
dual_Bridge_Series_C_Definition.md). The question posed there was whether C satisfies a
“residual law”: a modular part plus a controlled remainder, or an exact weight- 21 slash relation.
This paper consolidates seven internal reports (dated 17–18 October 2026; listed in §G.2) and
the errata recorded in the repository on 17 October 2026. It is written to be read without the
framework: all objects are defined from scratch below, and nothing in this paper depends on, or is
claimed to bear on, other parts of the framework.

What is not claimed. No claim is made about HQCC termination, about the twin prime con-
jecture, or about the Riemann hypothesis. We do not claim that C, Cthird or Ps is modular, mock
modular, or quantum modular as a scalar q-series; the quantum-modular statements concern the
fundamental-domain cone-sum vector of §6.

1.2     Category convention
Throughout, every result carries one of the following labels, and the label is repeated in every
section.

     Category A (proved, or exact). We always state the precise status, among: proved (written
      proof in the sources, not audited); proved, audited once (one internal audit pass, §18.2; in
      revision 3 this includes the items re-derived in the audit of Appendix B); exact finite verification
      (rational, integer, modular or cyclotomic arithmetic over a stated finite set, including exact
      ranks); conditional on a named cited theorem (stated each time) or on exact data produced
      by PARI/GP; modulo F1 (proved given the cited two-dimensional Euler–Maclaurin expansion
      F1).

     Category B: results whose only support is floating-point or multiprecision numerics (including
      SVD ranks, FFT character counts and fits), PSLQ recognitions, identifications by citation only,
      interpretations, analogies, patterns on finitely many cases, and conjectures.

Revision 4 convention. Revisions 1–3 allowed “numerically verified” and “PSLQ identification”
inside Category A. From revision 4 on, a statement whose only support is numerical is Category B.
Numerical checks reported inside a Category A box are supporting evidence for a statement that
is proved or exact. Items relabelled under this rule are listed in Appendix C.

                                                                        6
1.5      Summary of results
In one sentence: C and Cthird share √ the remainder Ps ; Ps is, up to theta and mock pieces, one
ray class partial theta series of Q( 35); its regulator-type singularity rules out every scalar mod-
ular/mock “residual law” (Baker); and the obstruction is resolved, not by a scalar law, but by
a vector-valued weight-1 quantum modular structure coming from a Siegel theta lift to weight-0
Maass forms, whose cocycle is computed in closed form. The precise statements are collected in §2.


2      Main results
Each theorem is followed by its exact status. Section references in brackets [Sn] are to the source
reports listed in §G.2.

Theorem A (Shared remainder). As formal q-series,

    (i) Cthird = q 8 f2,7,7 (−q 10 , −q 14 , q) + Ps ;

 (ii) C − Cthird = −Hs − q 8 f2,7,7 (−q 10 , −q 14 , q);

(iii) Θhol = q 8 f2,7,7 (−q 10 , −q 14 , q) + θ3 = Cthird +            n2 − R, and P           1
                                                               P
                                                               n≥1 q                   s = R + 2 (1 + θ3 );

 (iv) Cmixed = −Hs − q 8 f2,7,7 (−q 10 , −q 14 , q) − Caxis vanishes through q 293 but not identically:
      Cmixed = − 21 q 294 + 12 q 301 − q 330 + q 337 − q 368 + q 375 − · · · .

Status (Category A). (i) proved by reindexing [S1, A1]; (ii)–(iv) follow from (i) and the definitions
by elementary reindexing (§3.1; the reindexing for (iii) is written out in this consolidation); all identities
also checked with exact rational coefficients through q 3000 [S1, A1]. The first nonzero terms in (iv) are
an exact computation.

Theorem D (Gauss-mean pattern and zero set). For     √ gcd(h, m) = 1: G(h/m) = 0 if 2 | m or 5 | m;
|G(h/m)| = 1/m if gcd(m, 70) = 1; |G(h/m)| = 7/m if 7 | m and gcd(m, 10) = 1. Moreover
limt→0+ t S(e(x)e−t ) = I · G(x) for S ∈ {Ps , C, Cthird } and every rational x.

Status (Category A). Pattern: proved [S2, A4]; checked on all 3374 coprime pairs with m ≤ 105.
(Rev. 4 name note: the zero-set statement “G(h/m) = 0 iff 2 | m or 5 | m” is called “Theorem G” in
[S11]; its consolidated name is Theorem D. Theorem G of this paper is the Lewis–Zagier period formula;
Theorem Gs is the gs result.) Limit: Euler–Maclaurin argument [S1, A2, Lemma 3], numerically checked
to t = 6.25 · 10−4 .

Theorem E (Baker obstruction: scalar no-go). (i) (Additive.) There is no identity S = F + E
    with S ∈ {C, Cthird }, F in the Q-span M of §4 (holomorphic modular forms with alge-
    braic coefficients, signature-(1, 1) Zwegers holomorphic parts with rational characteristics,
    unary/partial/false theta series), and E(e−t ) = o(1/t).

 (ii) (Multiplicative.) For holomorphic modular forms g ̸≡ 0 and F of any weights, levels and
      multipliers, with complex coefficients, and any q r factor: Cthird · g ̸= F and C · g ̸= F .

(iii) (Scalar W7 .) For no k and no constant λ is C(τ ) − λ(7τ )−k C(−1/(7τ )) radially bounded at
      all rationals.

3     The real-quadratic decomposition and the ray class
3.1    Elementary identities (Theorem A)
Proof of Theorem A. (i) [S1, A1]. In q 8 f2,7,7 (−q 10 , −q 14 , q) put n = r + 1, k = s + 1. The signs
(−1)r+s from x = −q 10 , y = −q 14 cancel those in f , and on r, s ≥ 0 the exponent is 8 + 10r +
14s + r(r − 1) + 7rs + 72 s(s − 1) = Q− (n, k); this orthant gives Cthird . On r, s < 0, after (r, s) =
(−1 − n, −1 − k) the exponent is Q7 (n, k) with sign −1; this gives −Ps .
    (ii) is (i) combined with C = Ps −Hs . For (iii) (reindexing written for this consolidation): in the
second sum defining Θhol substitute (n,P    k) = (−n′ , −k ′ ) with n′ ≥ 0, k ′ ≥ 1; since Q− (−v) = Q7 (v)
                                                   2
it equals R. The first sum is Cthird + n≥1 q n (split k ≥ 1 and k = 0). Hence Θhol = Cthird +
         n2 − R. The k = 0 row of P gives P = R +                   n2 = R + 1 (1 + θ ), and then (i) gives
P                                                        P
   n≥1 q                                s       s             n≥0 q            2       3
Θhol = q 8 f + θ3 . (iv) is the definition combined with (ii).

Status (Category A). All identities in Theorem A were in addition checked with exact rational
coefficients through q 3000 ; the Hs summation box was enlarged by 30 with identical output [S1, A1].

Remark 3.1 (Origin of Cmixed ). At q 294 the point (21, −1) lies on the c2 -wall n + 21k = 0, has
w = 12 and lies outside the orthant, contributing − 12 . Points such as (22, −1) (q 330 ) and (−22, 1)
(q 337 ) lie in the Hs cones but outside the orthant. Thus “C = Caxis + Cthird ” holds only through
q 293 . The coefficient set {0, 1, 23 } holds through q 101 only (first coefficient 2 at q 102 , first 3 at q 144 ,
first 21 at q 294 ); through q 3000 the set is {0, 12 , 1, 32 , 2, 3}. The support {0, 1, 2, 4} mod 7 holds for all
terms since Q7 ≡ n2 (mod 7). (Category A, exact; repository erratum E1 of 17 Oct.)

                                                        10
   Consequently, every part of C and of Cthird other than Ps is a signature-(1, 1) mock/indefinite
theta object or a unary theta series, and the residual-law question is a question about Ps alone.
Note also that R is a cone sum of the indefinite form Q7 (positive only
                                                                      P on the closed orthant), so it
cannot be split by inclusion–exclusion into full lattice theta series: Z2 q Q7 diverges [S1, A1, F1].

3.3   Ray-class identification (Theorem B)
                                                                  √           √
Put (X, Y ) = 10ν ∈ (7, −2) + 10Z2 and β = (2X + 7Y ) + Y 35 √           ∈ Z[ 35] [S2, A2]. √  Then
Q(ν) = N(β)/400, β(γν) = εβ(ν), β runs over β0 + 10p2 with β0 = −2 35 and p2 = (2, 1 + 35),
and ν ∈ S + ⇔ β ≫ 0. With d′ = gcd((β0 ), 10p2 ) = p22 p5 and f = p2 p5 , the ideals a = (β)d′−1 are
exactly αp7 with α ≡ 1 mod × f and sgn α = (−, +): one narrow ray class K modulo f∞1 ∞2 . Totally
positive units ≡ 1 mod × f are εZ (ε ≡ 1 mod p2 and mod p5 ), so γ-orbits correspond bijectively
to ideals in K, and Na = 20Q7 (v) − 7. This yields Theorem B. Sanity check: the first norms are
13, 73, 133 = 7 · 19, 153 = 9 · 17, 173, . . . , all ≡ 13 mod 20.
Status (Category A). Proved: hand algebra with symbolic checks of Q = N(β)/400 and β(γν) = εβ
[S2, A2]. Not audited. The region-to-class bijection was also checked exactly against PARI’s independent
ideal enumeration up to norm 60 000 [S5, §5].

Lemma 4.1 (Zwegers parts carry no 1/t term [S1, A2, Lemma 2]). The holomorphic part of any
signature-(1, 1) indefinite theta ϑca,b
                                     1 ,c2

Sketch. The weight is +1 on a cone C and −1 on −C (walls contribute O(t−1/2 )). The mean of
e(B(ν, b)) over the coset is the same on C and −C, and Q is even, so the leading integrals cancel.

Status (Category A). Proved (symmetry argument; null cones excluded). Numerically t Hs → 0 (fit
−0.0002).

4.2   The additive obstruction (Theorem E(i))
Let M be the Q-span of: (i) q r f , r ∈ Q, f a holomorphic weight-1 form on some Γ(N ) with Q

(iii) q r times Zwegers holomorphic parts as in Lemma 4.1; (iv) unary, partial and false theta series
                ϵ an2 +bn with ψ periodic and Q-valued, ϵ ∈ {0, 1}, a > 0 [S1, A3].

Proof of Theorem E(i). Multiply by t and let t → 0+ . Class (i) contributes 2πβ with β ∈ Q
(algebraic expansion of f |S); (ii) contributes 0; (iii) contributes 0√by Lemma
                                                                             √ 4.1; (iv) contributes

or mean(ψ)/(2a) ∈ Q. So I = 2πβ+γ ′ with β, γ ′ ∈ Q,    √ i.e. log(6+  35)+4  35 iβ log(−1)−2

0, a nontrivial Q-linear relation among 1, log(6 + 35), log(−1) = iπ. Since log(6 + 35) (real,
nonzero) and iπ are Q-linearly independent, Baker’s theorem [1] says 1, log α1 , log α2 are Q-linearly
independent: contradiction.

Status (Category A). Proved, conditional on Baker [1] and on algebraicity of cusp expansions
(Shimura’s Q(ζN )-rationality / the q-expansion principle); neither re-derived, theorem numbers not
re-checked [S1, A3]. Lemma 1 of [S1] (the radial expansion) is now covered by the sector-uniform
Euler–Maclaurin argument R1 of §8.3.

Category B. Reading: the remainder of C is “weight-1 sized” at q → 1 with a regulator as coefficient;
weight- 21 false thetas are O(t−1/2 ) and cannot carry it. An Appell–Lerch formula for E or Ps with
algebraic data would likewise be excluded, but this needs the q → 1 asymptotics of Appell–Lerch sums,
which were not verified; that obstruction is conditional [S2, B3].


4.3   The multiplicative obstruction (Theorem E(ii))
Proof. Along q = e−t , F/g = c t−j e−α/t (1 + O(e−β/t )) times a power series in t, so only integer-step
powers of t occur; but Cthird has the nonzero term −aP t−1/2 and C the nonzero term aC t−1/2
(Table 1) [S1, A4; 2, A3].

Status (Category A). Proved. For C the nonvanishing of aC rests on its closed form (proved modulo
F1 since rev. 4; value 0.8291 . . .). This covers every space with k ∈ { 21 , 1, 32 , 2}, N | 140 or 280; no
Sturm-bound search was needed.


4.4   Gauss means and the scalar W7 obstruction
Proof of Theorem D (pattern), after [S2, A4]. If 2 | m, shift k 7→ k + m: (Q7 (n, k + m) −
Q7 (n, k))/m = 7n + 7k + 72 (m + 1) and the phase e(7h(m + 1)/2) = −1, so G = 0. If m is
odd, Q7 is m-periodic and 2 is invertible mod m; with n′ = n + 7 · 2−1 k, hQ7 ≡ h[n′2 − 35 2   7

Status (Category A). Zero/nonzero pattern and moduli: exact, by Theorem D (the floating-point
exponential sums, nonzero values ≥ 0.034 and zeros < 10−9 , agree).

Category B. Rev. 4 relabel: the phase values quoted above are floating-point (wide margin) and are
not separately proved.

Category B. The scalar failure is vector mixing: the leading profiles obey a vector-valued law (§5.1),
under which W7 -type maps mix components; this is not a contradiction with modularity of the vector
(erratum E3).

Remark 4.2 (Published sample values). The four “Fricke samples” of the framework (magni-
tudes 19.80, 1.82, 9.24, 20.90 at x = 1/7, 3/7, 5/7, 1) are not radial values of C, Cthird or Ps :
|C(0.995 e(x))| ≈ 19.5, 18.5, 19.9, 54.0 [S1, B1]. The 4-parameter “residual factor” fitted to 4 ratios
(interpolation determinant 36.14118 ̸= 0) is an interpolation, not a test [S1, A6].√The finite radial
targets are the singular coefficients I · G(x); e.g. I · G(1) = I and |I · G| = I/ 7 = 0.079153 . . .
at x = 1/7, 3/7, 5/7. The near-equality |sample(1)| = 20.90 ≈ I/(2t) = 20.89 is a numerical
coincidence (Category B: no evidential weight).


5     The theta lift and the vector-valued S/T law
5.1   Leading order: radial profiles
For α, β ∈ Q2 let Gα,β (x) be the mean over ν ∈ α + Z2 of e(xQ(ν) + B(ν, β)); this is the 1/t radial
profile (divided by the cone measure) of any cone sum over that coset and twist (leading-order
Euler–Maclaurin, as in Theorem D and R1). For rational α, β and rational x the summand is
periodic on α + Z2 , and the mean is the average over one period. Write A∗ = (A11 , A22 ) = (2, 7),
so 21 A−1 A∗ = ( 12 , 0).

Theorem 5.1 (Profile S/T law; revision 3). For all α, β ∈ Q2 and all x ∈ Q \ {0},

Status (Category A). Theorem 5.1: proved (rev. 3, this paper; written proof above, with the analytic
inputs being Poisson summation for Schwartz functions and the complex Gaussian Fourier transform).
Not externally refereed; it has had no audit pass separate from its derivation. Numerically verified
independently of the proof [S15]: S-law on all 140 labels at x ∈ {1, −1, 31 , − 23 } (560 tests, 280 with
nonzero value), on 13 labels at 12 values of x (both signs), and on 40 + 60 random rational pairs (α, β)
outside the family (3 + 60 with nonzero value): maximal deviation 3.3 · 10−15 ; T -law on all 140 labels:
5.6 · 10−16 ; with the signed factor x the ratio is exactly −1 at x < 0. Earlier finite checks [S2, A5], [S11]
are consistent.

    At the cusp pair (0, ∞) no exact vector-valued modular law with algebraic data can hold: at
x = 0 each cone sum has singular part IG/t ̸= 0, while at ∞ there is no q 0 term (Q has no
nonzero rational zeros since 35 is not a square); an exact law would force I ∈ πQ + Q, excluded
by Baker [S2, A5] (Category A, conditional on Baker). At order t−1/2 the quadrant-cone vector
fails the weight-1 S-law (|LHS|/|RHS| ∈ [0.49, 2.97] over 16 tests, either wall convention), while the
fundamental-domain vector has vanishing t−1/2 coefficients (≤ 5·10−15 ) [S2, A6] (Category B under
the rev. 4 convention: floating-point ratios and residuals; the second-order formula is a derivation
in the source).

The automorphy factor. With the holomorphic factor x the cocycle at x < 0 √         is unbounded (it
grows like 1/y); with the real factor |x|, the boundary value of EL (−1/z) + (z/ 35)ER (z) gives
the conjugate. Near 0, ψ(x) ≈ −0.0549i x2 + 0.0206 x3 (from x = 0.01, 0.05); ψ is not analytic at
0. Since T acts by phases and S, T generate, every hγ is real-analytic off γ −1 ∞ [S3, A4]. Example:
ψ(1) = 0.006883993760419869190321587 − 0.03825971221697307224258855 i [S6].
Category B. Interpretation: the 72-dimensional fundamental-domain vector is a vector-valued quantum
modular form of weight 1 in Zagier’s sense for the real automorphy factor |cx + d| (“weight ( 21 , 12 )”), i.e.
the f -function of a λ = 41 Maass vector, with exceptional set {0} for S; E itself is then a “renormalised”
weight-1 quantum modular object with the singular parts I · G(x) removed. The Lewis–Zagier period-
function reading [7] and the broader context of [3] are cited for context only.

7.2     The Eisenstein/cusp split (36 + 6) and the dihedral forms
Every one of the 140 components is exactly a ray class theta series modulo m = p32 p5 p7 ∞1 ∞2 (fit
over n coprime to 70 up to 54 880; max residual 4.4 · 10−14 over 280 fits). Expanding in characters:
mixed-type content ≤ 10−29 ; 116 components have cusp content ≤ 3 · 10−30 (pure Eisenstein); 24
components have Eisenstein content ≤ 8 · 10−31 (pure cusp) [S5, §3]. Function-space dimensions
(rev. 3; exact since rev. 4, Proposition 11.4). Since distinct frequencies give independent mode
functions, dim span{uα,β } is the rank of the label×frequency coefficient matrix. This rank is 42;
the Eisenstein components span 36 and the cusp components 6 dimensions, and both subspaces
are S- and T -invariant (residuals ≤ 8 · 10−16 ) [S14], recomputed from the explicit lift formula with
independent code at |Q| ≤ 12 and |Q| ≤ 25 (gap 6.4 vs. 6 · 10−15 ) [S15]. The earlier “rank 12 /
rank 60 in the 72-dimensional space” measured label rows of 21 (I + P ), which is not an S-invariant
decomposition; it is withdrawn. The 24 cusp components are the β = −a labels with 70α1 ∈
{7, 21, 49, 63} and 7 | denominator of α2 (indices 73, 75, . . . , 125 in [S5, §3.3] except 95, 107, 117, i.e.
24 of those 27 odd indices; the looser description “70α ∈ {(49, ∗), (63, ∗), (7, ∗), (21, ∗)}” gives 28
labels, the extra 4, namely 70α = (49, 56), (63, 42), (7, 28), (21, 14) (indices 71, 95, 107, 117), being
identically zero functions). In all, 11 labels (all with β = −a) give identically vanishing uα,β
                                                                 7 4         9 3        1 2         3 1
[S15]. This vanishing is exact: for the first five (α ∈ {( 10      , 5 ), ( 10 , 5 ), ( 10 , 5 ), ( 10 , 5 ), ( 12 , 0)}) the

                                                                   19
automorph γ, for the remaining six (α = ( 12 , 7j ), 1 ≤ j ≤ 6; also j = 0) the automorph −γ, maps
α + Z2 onto itself, commutes with γ 2 , leaves the kernel of the lift unchanged (it depends on ν evenly
and γ-covariantly, and the closed geodesic is γ-stable), and multiplies the phase e(B(ν, β)) by −1
(because, with g = ±γ the relevant automorph, g −1 β − β = µ ∈ L′ with B(α, µ) ≡ 12 ); so the sum
over ν modulo γ 2 equals its own negative. Checked exactly (rational arithmetic) over all 140 labels:
these are exactly 11 labels [S15]. (Category A.)

7.3    Function span, S and T spectra (revision 3)
Let V = span{uα,β (w)} ⊂ C140 be the span of the values of the vector u (equivalently the column
span of the coefficient matrix). Since u(−1/w) = Su(w) and u(w + 1) = T u(w), V is S, T -invariant,
and V ⊂ Fix(P ).

Proposition 7.1. (i) dim V = 42 = 36 + 6, V = VEis ⊕ Vcusp , both summands S, T -invariant.
(ii) On Fix(P ) (dim 72), S has eigenvalues ±1 with multiplicities 36/36 (tr S|Fix(P ) = 0); on V
they are 21/21, namely 18/18 on VEis and 3/3 on Vcusp . (iii) T has order 140. On Fix(P ) its
                                                   1
eigenvalues are e(r) with 36 distinct r ∈ 140        Z/Z, of multiplicities 1 (12 values), 2 (18 values) and
                   19 31 59 111 131 139
4 (6 values: r = 140 , 140 , 140 , 140 , 140 , 140 ). On VEis each of the 36 values occurs once; on Vcusp
the eigenvalues are the 6 values of multiplicity 4. Hence Fix(P ) = V ⊕ V ⊥ with a 30-dimensional
T -stable complement V ⊥ (multiplicities 0, 1, 2), which the lift functions do not realise. (Rev. 4
notation: revision 3 called this complement W ; here WP := Fix(P ) and the complement is V ⊥ .)
(iv) The 24 cusp characters form 12 σ-orbits of size 2 and give 12 distinct dihedral forms, all with
nonzero content; the 24 × 48 content matrix has rank 6, so the 12 forms occur only in 6 fixed linear
combinations.


                                                      20
Status (Category A). Since rev. 4, (i)–(iii) are exact: (i) by Proposition 11.4; (ii) by the exact
Gauss-sum traces of the local factors (Theorem 11.3), which give n± = d/2 on each of the four sectors;
(iii) order 140 exact (monomial T , rational phases), multiplicities from the exact phases and the sector
decomposition. In revisions 1–3, (i), (ii) on V and (iii) on V were numerical (singular-value gap 17.1 →
1.8 · 10−13 , invariance residuals ≤ 8 · 10−16 ) [S14], [S15]; these numerics agree.

Category B. (iv): the rank 6 of the content matrix is floating-point. Its dimension is explained exactly
by Vcusp = B1 ⊗ X − (dim = 6). The complement V ⊥ = B1 ⊗ X + ⊕ A3 ⊗ X − is killed by the lift exactly
(Proposition 11.4); the positive-frequency partners fα,β span the same V [S14]. The rev. 2 hypothesis
that V ⊥ is realised by weight-1 lifts is retired, since weight-1 vectors lie in Fix(−P ) (connection N7,
§12.1).


Consequence for ψ. Both representations of the scalar ψ involve only uL and uR , which are
Eisenstein; hence ψcusp ≡ 0, ψ = ψEis (Category A given the above). The 24 cusp rows of Φ are
nonzero (e.g. Φ73 (1) = 0.120446470003 + 0.176437422094 i); their identification as Lewis–Zagier
period functions of the cusp vector is Category B.

R2 (Fourier sum and Green integral commute): dominated convergence with frequencies |Q| ≥ n0 >
0 (n0 = 4/140 for uR , 0.35 for uL ) and O(X) γ-orbits with |Q| ≤ X [S7, §6.1]. R1 (sector-uniform

s/t + h t−1/2 + κ + O(|t|1/2 ) with the real-t coefficients; proved by order-3 two-variable Euler–
Maclaurin with every remainder bounded [S7, §6.2].

Theorem 11.3 (Tensor factorisation, s = 7). (i) Exactly, Slab = (F5 / 5) ⊗ (F7 / 7) ⊗ S2 and T =
T5 ⊗ T7 ⊗ T2 . (ii) ρ = ρ5 ⊗ ρ7 ⊗ ρ2 , where ρp is the Weil representation of (Z/p, qp ) with q5 = 2x2 /5
and q7 = x2 /7, and ρ2 = (e(3/4)S2−1 , T2 ) is a faithful representation of SL2 (Z/4) (image of order
48). Hence ρ factors through SL2 (Z/140) = SL2 (Z/4) × SL2 (F5 ) × SL2 (F7 ). (iii) ρ2 = A3 ⊕ B1 ,
where A3 is the span of the three even characteristics 00, 01, 10 (irreducible, T -spectrum {1, −1, i})
and B1 is the odd characteristic 11 (S, T 7→ e(1/4)). (iv) P = ρ(−I) = Z5 ⊗Z7 ⊗I4 , where Zp is the
reflection x 7→ −x. Hence WP = (W5+ ⊗ W7+ ⊕ W5− ⊗ W7− ) ⊗ C4 ; the mixed local parities are absent.
The restrictions are 16W5+ + 12W5− , 12W7+ + 8W7− and 18(A3 + B1 ). (v) tr ρ5 (S)|W ± = (−1, 0),
                                                                                            5
tr ρ7 (S)|W ± = (0, −1), tr ρ2 (S)|A3 = −i, tr ρ2 (S)|B1 = i, so tr S = 0 on each of the four sectors
            7
below and n± = 36/36 on WP .

Proof outline. Every entry of Slab is one term e(r)/ 35 with r ∈ Q, and T is monomial, so (i)
is a finite identity over Q(ζ140 ), checked exactly [S18]. For (ii), (Fp Tp )3 = c Zp exactly in Z[ζp ]
with c2 = 125 (p = 5) and c2 = −343 (p = 7); with Gauss’s sign of the quadratic Gauss sum
this gives the scalars γ5 = −1, γ7 = e(3/4), so ρp is exactly the Weil representation of qp ; by the


                                                   25
congruence theorem for Weil representations of finite quadratic modules of odd level [16], ρp factors
through SL2 (Z/p). ρ2 satisfies S 4 = I, (ST )3 = S 2 = −I, T 4 = I exactly, and the pair group
                                                                                              −1
(ρ2 (g), g mod 4) has exactly 48 elements. The scalars cancel in the product, which equals (Slab , T ).
(iv) and (v) follow from the local Gauss sums.

Status (Category A). Exact (Fractions and cyclotomic arithmetic) [S18], conditional on the cited
congruence theorem for the odd local factors [16] (hypotheses checked: exact match with the Weil
formulas, odd level). The earlier numerical certificates of [S17] (congruence words Mp , character residuals
≤ 3 · 10−11 ) are Category B and are superseded by this exact proof. Correction (C4): [S26] listed the
local forms as x2 /5 and 3x2 /7 (raw residues of the 140th-root exponent a). The true local exponents
satisfy a = 35x4 + 28x5 + 20x7 , i.e. x4 ≡ 3a mod 4, x5 ≡ 2a mod 5, x7 ≡ 6a mod 7, and the forms are
2x2 /5 and x2 /7. Since 2 is a non-square mod 5 and 3 is a non-square mod 7, the old forms are not
isometric to the true ones.

Proposition 11.4 (V is the matched-sector space; local reflections). Let√X ± = W5± ⊗ W7± . At
s = 7: (i) negation acts on u′ as Z5 ⊗ Z7 ⊗ I4 , and the unit γ (ε = 6 + 35) acts as I5 ⊗ Z7 ⊗
diag(1, 1, 1, −1); (ii) V = Fix(N ) ∩ Fix(γ) = A3 ⊗ X + ⊕ B1 ⊗ X − , of dimension 36 + 6 = 42, and
the lift kills V ⊥ = B1 ⊗ X + (12) ⊕ A3 ⊗ X − (18); (iii) VEis = A3 ⊗ X + and the cuspidal part of V is
Vcusp = B1 ⊗ X − . The 24 cusp components of §7 are exactly the nonzero labels with (ϵ, δ) = (1, 1),
and the 11 identically vanishing labels are exactly the 6 inconsistent orbits (x5 x7 = 0, ϵ = δ = 1);
(iv) each of the 42 classes is a free ⟨N, γ⟩-orbit (sizes 1, 2, 4: 3, 15, 24 classes), with proportionality
constants ±1 in the u′ basis; (v) the local reflections Z5 , Z7 and Z2 := diag(1, 1, 1, −1) commute
with ρ. Their signs (Z5 , Z7 , Z2 ) are (+, +, +) on VEis , (−, −, −) on Vcusp , (+, +, −) on B1 ⊗ X +
and (−, −, +) on A3 ⊗ X − . Thus Z5 Z7 = +1 on WP (this is P ) and Z7 Z2 = +1 exactly on V (this
is γ). S commutes with all three, and n± = d/2 on every joint eigenspace.

Proof outline. (i) The identities u−α,−β = uα,β and uγα,γβ = uα,β hold as identities of functions,
and their actions on u′ are computed exactly. (ii) Hence V ⊆ Fix(N ) ∩ Fix(γ), which has dimension
42. The exact Fourier matrix (sums of 4900th roots of unity, |Q| ≤ 25) has rank 42 modulo a prime
above ℓ = 29401 ≡ 1 mod 4900; rank cannot increase under reduction, so dim V ≥ 42. (iii) The
constant-term vector is δx=0 ⊗ (e00 + e01 ), a pure tensor with 2-adic part in A3 ; its cyclic span is
A3 ⊗ X + (the argument is that of Theorem 11.6(iii)). The cuspidal functions of the span are those
orthogonal to VEis (the λ = 41 constant-term criterion [S20]); by unitarity this is B1 ⊗ X − .

Status (Category A). Exact [S18]: (i), (ii), (iv), (v) and the label statements of (iii). The mechanism
that kills V ⊥ is the γ phase flip: the unit acts by −1 at 7 and by −1 on the odd characteristic, the
analogue of the exact vanishing of the 11 labels (R3.4). (iii) uses the cited congruence theorem and
the standard λ = 41 constant-term criterion. This answers the rev. 3 open items on the 30-dimensional
complement and on the structure behind the 6 cusp combinations.

Category B. Calling Z5 , Z7 , Z2 the Atkin–Lehner operators W5 , W7 , W4 on the lifted forms (not checked
on the forms). The Hecke-character reading: a character contributes only if ψ(ε) = ψ(−1) = 1; genus
characters are even at the ramified primes and land in A3 ⊗ X + ; characters odd at 5 and 7 are not genus
characters, hence not σ-invariant, and give Maass cusp forms in B1 ⊗ X − [S18, §4]. The identification
of Vcusp with specific dihedral Hecke eigenforms rests on the floating fits of §7.

with local factors certified as stated [S21]. (iv): the square root and the half-cone bound are exact

11.4    Two cusp notions: the family span and the vector uL
The family statement above concerns the whole span V . Whether the single vector uL = ua,0 has
cusp content is a different question; the two must be kept apart.

Proposition 11.8 (Cusp criterion for uL ). Let G = Cl+           M ∞1 ∞2 with M = 2dc, d = 2(s − 2),
        2
ns = c D0 ; let T ⊂ G be the class set of the coprime part of uL , S = Stab(T ), j = [−1],
Ḡ = G/(S + ⟨j⟩), H = (1 − σ)G and H̄ its image in Ḡ. (i) The coprime part of uL is Eisenstein
(genus-pure) iff H̄ = 1, i.e. iff σ acts trivially on Ḡ; the number of non-genus pure characters is then
|Ḡ|−|Ḡσ |. (ii) For odd s with c = 1 (ns squarefree): |Ḡ| = hφ(s−2); if |Ḡσ | = 2ω(s) φ(s−2), then uL
                        √
is Eisenstein iff h(Q( ns )) = 2ω(s) , and the number of non-genus characters is φ(s − 2)(h − 2ω(s) ).
Among the odd s ≤ 121 with c = 1 (class numbers certified with bnfcertify) this predicts Eisenstein
only at s = 5, 7, 13, 15. (iii) Verdicts. Eisenstein: s = 5, 7, 9, 13, 15, 16, 20, 24 (at 9, 16, 24 on every
tested part, Proposition 11.9). Cusp content: s = 11, 14, 17, 18, 19, 21, 22, 23, 25, 29, the odd s from
31 to 41, and s = 53, 55, 57. Open (predicted cusp): s = 32, 34, 36, 40. (iv) The rule “uL has cusp
content iff h+ ̸= #genera” is false: s = 14, 17, 18, 22, 23 have h+ = #genera and cusp content (all
with H̄ of order 2). (v) The two notions differ: dcusp > 0 at s = 5, 7, 13, 15, where uL is Eisenstein.
The uL verdict is class-group data (H̄) on top of the local structure.

identities, checked to q 400 at 27 values of s [S22]. (ii): modulo F1. The integer orders vanish by the

unary theta contributes only half-integer orders) [S22, §4.2]; the extended computation [S23] re-derives

argument. The closed forms come from symbolic Euler–Maclaurin with exact Bernoulli-sum validation
for s = 5..80, 99, 100, 151, 223, 300 [S23]; the polynomials Σ3/2 , Σ5/2 above are factorisations of the

for rev. 4). ∆-square list: exact search. (vi): modulo F1 and Hecke’s theorem [S22, §5].

Category B. Supporting numerics: H3/2 against small-t fits at 9 values of s (3–4 digits); H5/2 only
at s = 6, 8. Fits at x ̸= 0 (x = 12 , 31 for s even or 3 | s) are stable but not closed; the fits at x = 12 ,
s = 7, 9, 10 and x = 31 , s = 7, 10 are unstable and are not quoted. The modular completion of 12 q c0 ϑ is
by citation (Zwegers [13], Cor. 2.9). The pre-registered prediction PB5 of [S22] (a rational t1 term) was
refuted (the term is 0).

12.1    Connections
The labels N1–N11 are those of [S16]; the six used here are listed below.
N1. One improper automorph (Category A).
  The involution φ(n, k) = (n, −2n−k −1) of Lemma E (proof of Theorem A.1) equals −γRs , and
  Rs a = −a. So Lemma E, Lemma R, the automorph R of [S26] and H5 ≡ 0 come from a single
  symmetry. Rs c1 = −c2 holds only at s = 5, which is why H5 ≡ 0 and Q(c1 ) = Q(c2 ) there.
  Adding Rs to ⟨γ, −I⟩ gives no new label relations for any s = 5, . . . , 25 (exact orbit counts).

N4. The factor 20 (identity Category A; its meaning Category B).
  The denominator of c0 is 4(s − 2)/ gcd(s, 8) = 20, 3, 36 at s = 7, 8, 11, which is the ideal-norm
  factor cs in Na = cs Q of [S10]. For odd s it equals −B(c1 , c2 ) = −8Q(c1 ) = 4(s − 2) of the Hs
  cone (at s = 8 the two differ: 3 versus 24); equivalently Qs = (Na + s)/(4(s − 2)) for odd s.

N5. Residue and Pell unit (Category A).

13     Revision 5: cocycle closure, the uL criterion, sectors and s = 98
       (18 October 2026)
This section adds the results of four reports written after revision 4: [S28] (2026-10-18-Ope
n-Algebraic.md), [S29] (2026-10-18-Open-Analytic.md), [S30] (2026-10-18-uL-Criteri
on-Closure.md) and [S31] (2026-10-18-Cocycle-Closure.md). Category convention as in
§1.2: Category A means proved, or exact (including exact finite computation); floating-point-only
results are Category B. All new labels are numbered within this section. None of them is a lettered
theorem, none is “Theorem L” (the framework’s Parity Dominance Theorem), and none collides
with the framework labels L1–L7. No claim is made concerning HQCC termination, the twin prime
conjecture or the Riemann hypothesis; CORE FREEZE is respected.

13.1    F1 removed: the two-dimensional Euler–Maclaurin expansion
Proposition 13.1 (Cone Euler–Maclaurin [S29], §2). For every rational x, the shifted-lattice cone
sums of e−tQ that make up Hs (e(x)e−t ) and EL (x + h) have a full asymptotic expansion in powers
of t1/2 . The coefficients are area terms, edge Bernoulli sums with the half-weight rule B̃r at the
edges, and corner terms. The remainders are explicit integrals of periodic Bernoulli functions and
are bounded uniformly in every sector | arg t| ≤ π/2 − δ.

Status (Category A). Proved in [S29], §2: a one-dimensional lemma (Zagier’s asymptotic lemma
with the Euler–Maclaurin remainder), a quadrant lemma, and a decomposition of the closed cone into
translated quadrants. The sector-uniform form is [S31], §2.2. Consequence: the qualifier “modulo F1”
in Theorem 12.1, Theorem C and Table 1 is removed as far as the expansion is concerned. Any separate
Hecke input that is listed for the C closed forms is unaffected.

13.5     The uL criterion
Notation of Proposition 11.8. Here W ⊂ (O/M )× is the unit stabiliser of β0 + L (unrelated to
WP ), S0 is the coprime-part stabiliser and ψ(λ) = [(λ)].
Theorem 13.8 (Coset property [S30], §3, §3A). For every s and every c-part c, Tc + ψ(W) = Tc ,
and S0 = ψ(W). Hence every Tc is a union of S0 -cosets.

Status (Category A). The lemma Tc + ψ(W) = Tc is elementary. S0 = ψ(W) follows from the local
condition Rp = Wp , proved for all s and p in [S30], §3A: it reduces to the fact that ratios of exact-
valuation elements of β0 + L lie in the multiplier order of L. The cases are p ∤ f ; odd p | c with p | s or
p | s − 2; and p = 2 in each 2-adic case. Also checked exactly: global S0 = ψ(W) at s = 5, . . . , 44, and
Rp = Wp at s ≤ 150.

Theorem
    p    13.9 (Squarefree criterion [S30], §1). Let s be odd with s(s − 2) squarefree, and h =
h(Q( s(s − 2))). Then only the coprime c-part occurs, |Ḡσ | = 2ω(s) φ(s − 2), and uL is Eisenstein
⇐⇒ h = 2ω(s) . The number of non-genus characters is φ(s − 2)(h − 2ω(s) ).

Status (Category A). The proof uses Chevalley’s ambiguous class number formula (ray form, unit-
norm index 1) and, independently, Gauss genus theory; these are classical theorems applied to exact
data. The exact cross-check covers 47 values s ≤ 151: Eisenstein only at s = 5, 7, 13, 15.


       verdict (5 ≤ s ≤ 44)   s
       Eisenstein             5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 12, 13, 15, 16, 20, 24, 28
       CUSP                   11, 14, 17, 18, 19, 21, 22, 23, 25, 26, 27 and every s with 29 ≤ s ≤ 44


Status (Category A). Verdicts are per s over all (infinitely many) c-parts, using Theorem 13.8.
Coverage at s = 32, 34, 36, 40 was completed by enumeration to B = 200000, and all four are CUSP. At
s = 24 there are no mixed-prime parts for any exponents (a local proof), and the parts (1, 12), (12, 1),
(1, 16), (16, 1) are genus-pure [S30], §2.

13.6    Sectors: parity, the orbit lemma and prime squares
Proposition 13.10 (Odd-parity sectors are cusp [S28], Item 5). If ρ(T ) has no eigenvalue 1
on a ρ-invariant subspace σ, the projection of the lift onto σ is cuspidal.   Consequently dcusp ≥
(s − 1)(s − 3)/4 wherever the B1 sector lies in V , and dEis ≤ 3 (pe + 1)/2 whenever no odd
                                                                   Q
parity is carried only by primes with e ≥ 2 (every tested odd s ≤ 99; the first undecided cases are
s = 225, 1225).
                                                                                                  Q e
Status (Category A). Proved: no constant term at any cusp. Category B: equality dEis = 3           (p +
1)/2, i.e. that the all-even sector carries no cusp content.

Proposition 13.11 (Parity fact [S28], 17:38 update (a)). For every odd s, every ∆ label has even
second coordinate x2 . Hence Zall acts as negation on ∆, fixes every ⟨γ, −1⟩-orbit, and every sector
with εall = −1 vanishes.

Status (Category A). Symbolic: D·A−1 = −2 adjA gives even second coordinates on L′ ; a = (s2 , −2s);
D is even.

Proposition 13.12 (Corrected orbit lemma [S28], 15:50 update (1)). For every odd s,
cε (O0 , qmin ) = (1 + εall )NO0 (qmin ), where O0 = {±ν0 , ±γν0 } and ν0 = (s, −2). This is nonzero
on every sector with εall = +1. The original form of the lemma (no extra reduced classes) is false:
extra classes occur at s = 27, 245, 363, 847, 2025, . . . (16 cases with s ≤ 30001), but they never land
in ZS O0 .

Status (Category A). Symbolic (Nagell bound, γ 2 ν0 ≡ ν0 , stabiliser {1, Zall }, exclusion of extra
classes). Also checked exactly for odd s ≤ 401 and at all 16 exceptions. At s = 27 the sector run
certifies dim V = 702 (exact), in agreement with the rev. 4 table. Combined with Proposition 13.11, the
matched sectors with εall = +1 have a nonzero lift. Injectivity therefore follows wherever the sector is
irreducible (squarefree s(s − 2)). Open: dim V = s(s − 1) when e ≥ 2.

Proposition 13.13 (Prime-square sectors [S28]). The isotropic projector Π splits each sector into
old and new pieces. Exactly:

                              s   (+, +) old   (+, +) new    (−, −) new    total
                             9           12            48             12     72
                            11           18            72             20    110
                            25           36           432            132    600

Every piece is injective. The ν0 coefficient is nonzero on every new piece and zero on every old
piece.

Status (Category A). Exact integer ranks (flint). Open: a uniform proof, irreducibility of the pieces,
and a uniform leading vector for old pieces. Building ρ on the old piece is blocked, so the irreducibility
test was not run.


13.7    The 2-adic gap and s = 98
Let s = 2m2 with m odd and g = (γ + I)/(2m), as in Theorem 11.6(iv).
Proposition 13.14 (Complement trace [S28], 17:38 update (b)). For every odd m: #Fix(g) =
2(m − 1) and #Fix(−g) = 2(m + 1) on the complement, tr(g | Fix(N, γ)) = 2m, and the g = −1
eigenspace X of Fix(N ) has dimension m(m − 1).

                                                     36
Status (Category A). A(g ∓ 1)−1 is integral, and γ = 1 on the complement C. Exact at m = 3, 5, 7
as well.

Proposition 13.15 (Half-cone relations [S28], 18:51 update §2). If g acts on M7 -classes (more
generally on the classes of Z2 + Zt) and m is prime, the span of the half-cone g-relations ec − egc
is exactly δiso ⊗ X, of dimension m(m − 1).

Status (Category A). Proved for prime m, given that g is consistent on the classes. That consistency
is checked exactly only at m = 3, 5, 7. At m = 3, 5 the exact half-cone kernel equals δiso ⊗ X vector by
vector. Open: g-consistency for all odd m, and composite m (the m-part is not cyclic). In general the
kernel can be larger than the g-relations; see Theorem 13.16.

Theorem 13.16 (dim V (98) = 2406 [S28], 18:44 and 18:51 updates). At s = 98 (m = 7), dim V =
2406. The kernel of the lift on the 98-dimensional 7-adic old space has dimension 44. It is spanned
by the 42 = m(m − 1) g-relations and two h-relations, R1 (at q = 11/12) and R2 (at q = 1/4).
Here h = (g + I)/4 ∈ SO(A)(Q), with h2 = g and hT Ah = A. The half-cone upper bound 2408 is
not sharp.

Status (Category A). Lower bound: the exact rank is 2406 (flint, Qmax = 40; old-piece ranks are
stable to Qmax = 130). The new pieces are injective, and all 44 missing dimensions lie in the old
                                                T7     k             2
space. Upper bound: take the overlattice
                                        M =  k=0 h M7 , M7 = Z + Zt0 , with basis (4, 0), (1, 1/7),
                                         32 64
[M7 : M ] = 4 and Gram matrix (2Q)                (even, det −3072). Exact HNF gives hM = M and
                                         64 32
hM ♯ = M ♯ . Moreover h maps the + set of Ri bijectively onto its − set as M -cosets (32 +   √ 32 for R1 ,
16 + 16 for R2 ). The sets are γ-invariant (γ = h4 ). Since h has positive eigenvalues 2 ± 3, it maps
fundamental cones for ⟨γ⟩ to fundamental cones, so NS +√(q) = NS − (q) for all q, and the theta identities
hold. The same argument with g on M7 (eigenvalues 7± 48) proves the 42 g-relations. The 44 relations
are independent (exact rank 44), so dim V ≤ 2450 − 44 = 2406.

Category B. Prediction (not computed): extra h-relations beyond m(m − 1) occur exactly when 2m + 2
is a square, i.e. m = 2k 2 − 1 (m = 7, 17, 31, . . . ). At m = 17 (s = 578), h = (g + I)/6 exists, but the
number of extra relations was not computed, so no number is claimed. The general gap formula for
s = 2m2 is therefore open, and m(m − 1) is not the gap in general.


13.8    s = 64, cusp-sector bounds and Atkin–Lehner
Proposition 13.17 (s = 64 [S28], 15:10 (d) and 15:50 (3)). dim WP (64) = 1986. The 2-adic cyclic
span d2 at k = 6 equals 36, so the 2-adic factor of the function span is 36 · 16 = 576 at s = 64.

Status (Category A). WP is an exact count. For the upper bound, d2 ≤ #O(∆2 )-orbits = 42, minus
a 6-dimensional S, T -invariant subspace Y ⊥ δ0 that is verified exactly in Z[ζ64 ], leaving 36. The lower
bound 36 is exact. This settles the rev. 4 open item “d2 (k, v) for k ≥ 6” at k = 6 only. The value
2k−1 + 4 for general k remains Category B.
   Atkin–Lehner (partial). The 24 cusp characters at s = 7 have conductor of norm 140
(ramified at p2 , p5 , p7 ). The vector-valued components are Γ(140)-modular, and the scalar newforms
have level 1402 = 19600, so the two levels are consistent (Category A, by reconciliation). The global
root numbers are non-real (Category B, numerical), so the Zp act by pseudo-eigenvalues of modulus
1. Open (blocked): the local split at 5 and 7,√i.e. the local root numbers ε(ψp ). This needs the
additive character on Op (inverse different of Q( 35)) and the Langlands λ-factor convention, which
have not been implemented.

14.1   Symmetry audit of the label operators
Proposition 14.1 (Operator audit [S32]). In the label family for odd s, build each label map
with the convention Y [i, j(i)] = e(phi ), which is that of the family’s permutation matrix. Then
neg = ρ(−I) = S 2 , and neg, γ, Zp (p | s) and Zq (q | s − 2) all commute exactly with S and T .

Status (Category A). Exact exponent arithmetic modulo K = 2D2 at s = 5, 7, 9, 11, 13, 15, 25.
The transposed convention X[j(i), i], which was used by commutant.py, krylov irr.py and
sec sparse/sec valid, breaks neg and Zq . γ and Zp pass in both conventions.
Consequences. Every rev. 4/rev. 5 claim that uses neg or Zq is sound :

14.2   Prime-square sectors
Let s = p2 with p an odd prime. Write Wp± for the even and odd parts of the Weil representation
of Z/p2 (or of Z/q), and A3 for the irreducible 3-dimensional 2-adic factor (rev. 4).

Theorem 14.2 (Prime-square structure [S33], 21:51 update). Assume (C1) Theorem 11.5 and (C2)
CRT surjectivity, both from rev. 4, and (C3) the cited Nobs–Wolfart / Cliff–McNeilly–Szechtman

                                                    38
decomposition Wp+ = 1 ⊕ R, with R irreducible of dimension (p2 − 1)/2, and Wq± irreducible for
primes q. Let ε = (εq )q|s−2 , with Z(s) = +. Then:
                                           ε                                              ε
  (i) The sector is Σε = Wp+ ⊗ ( q Wq q ) ⊗ A3 . Its p-local invariants are 1 ⊗ ( q Wq q ) ⊗ A3 .
                                     N                                             N
      Summed over the ε with Z(s−2) = +, these have total dimension 3(s − 1)/2.
                                                                        ε
 (ii) If s − 2 is squarefree, then Σε = (p-local invariants) ⊕ R ⊗ ( q Wq q ) ⊗ A3 is a sum of two

Status (Category A). Conditional on (C3), which is cited and not re-proved; (C1) and (C2) are
Category A in rev. 4. Irreducibility of the complement follows from the outer-tensor argument. Caveats:
if s − 2 is composite, the sector splits by prime sign (see s = 121 below). If q 2 | s − 2, the complement
is reducible.

Proposition 14.3 (Exact splits [S32,33]). The “(+, +)” sectors split as follows: s = 9: 12 + 48;
s = 11: 18 + 72; s = 25: 36 + 432; s = 49: 72 + 1728. In each case the commutant is 2 and
the smaller piece is exactly the p-local invariant subspace. The (−, −) sectors at s = 9, 11, 25
(dimensions 12, 20, 132) are irreducible, and the mixed sectors vanish.

Status (Category A). The sectors are built in the correct convention, with an exact invariance cer-
tificate (0 failures). The commutant is computed mod ℓ on an exactly verified basis. It is lifted to C
using ℓ ∤ |Mp2 (Z/N )|; this divisibility is machine-checked (e.g. s = 49: ℓ = 1272914161). Standard input,
cited: a Weil representation factors through Mp2 (Z/ord T ). An outline of this is in [S33] (22:10): for
γ ∈ Γ(N ), the cited Borcherds/Scheithauer Γ0 (N ) formula gives ρD (γ) = χD (γ)I. It remains cited.
After Proposition 14.4, the splits at s = 9, 25 need neither this nor (C3). The s = 9, 25, 49 splits agree
with Theorem 14.2(ii), where s − 2 = 7, 23, 47 is prime.

Category B. s = 121, factor dimensions only (no label-space run). The factor data are exact mod
ℓ: Z/121 even = 1 ⊕ R60 , odd 60 irreducible; Z/7: 4, 3; Z/17: 9, 8. Combined with the cited tensor
factorisation, they predict p-local invariants of 108 in (+, +, +) and 72 in (+, −, −), for a total of
180 = 3(s − 1)/2.

Proposition 14.4 ((C3) for small p; non-squarefree s − 2 [S33], 22:10 update). For p ∈
{3, 5, 7, 11, 13, 59}, the even Weil representation of Z/p2 is 1 ⊕ R with R irreducible, and the odd
part is irreducible. Together with (C1), this gives the (+, +) decompositions at s = 9, 25, 49 in
characteristic 0 directly, without the ℓ ∤ |Mp2 | lift.

Status (Category A). The mod-ℓ commutant is 2 (even part) or 1 (odd part), on exactly verified bases,
for both square classes. Semicontinuity bounds the characteristic-0 commutant above by the mod-ℓ one.
The explicit invariant line δpZ/p2 bounds it below by 2. The general-p case remains cited (Nobs–Wolfart

14.3    h-relations at m = 2k 2 − 1
Proposition 14.5 ([S33], post-push erratum (d)). Let m = 2k 2 −1 and h = (g+I)/(2k). The extra

h-eigenspaces (22:10 update). On Y (the old g-orbits together with their h-images), h is an

Category B. The general count k(k − 1) (three data points; no proof). Lower bounds at m = 17, 31

                                                                                         P

14.5    Local root numbers at s = 7
Proposition 14.10 ([S35]). For the 24 cusp characters, the local root numbers εv (p2 , p5 , p7 , ∞) are
exact elements of Q(ζ840 ) of modulus 1. εp2 is a 6th root of unity; εp5 is never a root of unity. The
global root numbers have non-monic minimal polynomials (e.g. 5t8 + 6t4 + 5), so they are not roots
of unity. The sector signs are products of local root numbers: Zp = ψp (−1) = ε(ψp )ε(ψp−1 ) = −1
(p = 5, 7), and Sp2 = ωπp (−1). They are not individual local root numbers.

Status (Category A). The local factors (Tate’s formulas) and the character-side identities are exact.

Category B. v εv = W against PARI’s numerical root number (≤ 2.7 · 10−63 ). Wp ↔ Sp holds only
               Q
up to a fixed unit (the Atkin–Li normalisation, cited and not re-derived).

15.1     Proposition K1: the extra h-relations at m = 2k 2 − 1
Fix k ≥ 2, m = 2k 2 − 1, s = 2m2 , and let g = (γ + I)/(2m) and h = (g + I)/(2k) be as
in Theorem 11.6(iv) and Theorem 13.16. Let rankg and rankh be the ranks of the orbit-sum
maps F → Fun(⟨X, −1⟩-orbits on M ♯ /M ), X ∈ {g, h}, with F = Fun(Mt♯ /Mt )⟨γ,−1⟩ , as defined in
hcount.py [S33].

Proposition K1 ([S37], §1). rankg = m(m + 1) and rankh = m(m + 1) − k(k − 1). Hence the
number of extra h-relations is exactly k(k − 1) for every k ≥ 2. Moreover dim F = 2m2 = s.

Status (Category A). Consequence of Proposition K1. The equal-kernel statement ker+ = ker− of

Dimension consequences (Category A as upper bounds; sharpness open). Proposition K1
is a statement about the lattice-level rank rankh , which is a proved upper bound for the old
lift rank. Every h-relation is a proved theta relation (the argument of Theorem 13.16), so for
s = 2m2 , m = 2k 2 − 1, the dimension bounds obtained by subtracting the k(k − 1) extra relations
are proved upper bounds: dim V (98) ≤ 2406 (equality, Theorem 13.16) and dim V (578) ≤ 83532
(Proposition 14.5), and likewise at every m = 2k 2 −1. Whether these bounds are sharp, i.e. whether
there are no further relations (equality with the actual theta rank) at m = 17, 31, . . . , is not proved.
This supersedes the Category B status of the k(k − 1) count in §14.3.

15.2    The lattice origin of ∆ = (s − 2)(7s − 32)
Let A = ( 2s ss ) be the Gram matrix of Q2 (n, k) = n2 + snk + 2s k 2 , so det A = −s(s − 2), and let
c1 = (−1, 1), c2 = (−4, 1) (the locked choice) and e1 = (1, 0).

Proposition 15.2 ([S37], §2). (i) 7s − 32 = −2Q(c2 ). Indeed c2 = c1 − 3e1 , B(c1 , e1 ) = s − 2,
    Q(e1 ) = 1, and −2Q(c2 ) = (s − 2) + 6(s − 2) − 18 = 7s − 32.

 (ii) v := (3s, s − 8) = JAc2 (J the rotation by 90◦ ) spans c⊥
                                                              2 , B(c2 , x) = det(x, v), and u2 = v/g
      with g = gcd(s − 8, 3s) = gcd(24, s − 8).

(iii) 2Q(v) = det A · 2Q(c2 ) = s(s − 2)(7s − 32), so Q2 (u2 ) = s∆/(2g 2 ).

 (iv) For any w with det(w, u2 ) = 1, the residual coefficient in the Gauss phase of Proposi-
      tion 14.8(iii) is det A/(4Q(u2 )) = −g 2 /(2(7s − 32)) = Q(πw), where π is the B-orthogonal
      projection onto Rc2 along u2 .

 (v) ∆ = 4Q(c1 )Q(c2 ) = B(c1 , c2 )2 + det Gram(c1 , c2 ) = 16(s − 2)2 − 9s(s − 2), with B(c1 , c2 ) =
     −4(s − 2) and det(c1 , c2 )2 = 9.

Status (Category A). All identities are symbolic in s (delta lattice.py). The degenerate prime
cases of Proposition 14.8(iv) are read off: p | 7s − 32 means that c2 is isotropic mod p (the residual sum
collapses to a delta), and p | s − 2 is the c1 analogue. No physical interpretation is offered.

Proposition 15.5 (Status of c2 = (−4, 1) [S37], §5(c), §6.2). c2 = (−4, 1) is a definition: it was
locked at s = 7 in Residual Bridge Series C Definition.md, §1, and frozen as s varies (§12).
Within the class c ∈ c1 + Ze1 (second coordinate b = 1) at s = 7: Q(ca ) < 0 iff a ∈ {1, . . . , 6};
the closed orthant lies strictly inside the w = 1 region iff a ≥ 4; and the Cmixed vanishing order is
maximal (first nonzero term q 294 ) exactly for the M -class {3, 4}. Together these give a = 4 uniquely
among b = 1. Among all primitive (−a, b) with b ≤ 6 it is not unique, even up to M : the vanishing
order grows as a/b → s/2, and the optimum under the Cmixed criterion is the axes vector (−7, 2).
For s ̸= 7 the frozen choice is not forced (the deepest b = 1 class is a ≈ s/2).

15.7    Weil-representation inputs computed exactly
Proposition 15.15 ([S37], §3). The even and odd parts of the Weil representation of Z/M have
the following (dimension, commutant): M = 47: (24, 1), (23, 1); M = 59: (30, 1), (29, 1); M = 71:
(36, 1), (35, 1); M = 172 : (145, 2), (144, 1); M = 192 : (181, 2), (180, 1); M = 232 : (265, 2),
(264, 1). Hence for q = 47, 59, 71 both parts are irreducible, and for p = 17, 19, 23 the even part of
Z/p2 is 1 ⊕ R with R irreducible and the odd part is irreducible.

Status (Category A). Commutants computed mod ℓ ≡ 1 (mod M ) on exactly verified bases, both
square classes (weil cyclic.py); the characteristic-0 commutant is bounded above by the mod-ℓ one,
and for Z/p2 below by 2 via the invariant line δpZ/p2 . With Proposition 14.4, (C3) is now Category A
                                                                                  ±
for every prime q | s − 2 and every p2 occurring at s = 9, 25, 49 (in particular W47 irreducible, used for
                                  2      2      2

number not verified).

15.8    Correction remark
Remark 15.16 (Refinement of the role of ∆). An earlier statement, that ∆ = (s − 2)(7s − 32)
constrains the S2 -side expansions intrinsically, is refined as follows: only the factor (s−2) = −2Q(c
                                                                                                    √1 )
is intrinsic (given c1 , which is forced by the wall n = 0). The factor 7s − 32 = −2Q(c2 ) and the ∆
terms are properties of the locked choice c2 = (−4, 1) (Remark 15.6; Category A). The universal
piece Re does not see c2 through t13/2 (Proposition 15.9). The locked object is unchanged.

15.9    Open problems after revision 7
 1. Sharpness of the K1 bounds. Prove (or refute) that there are no relations beyond the g-
    and h-relations at m = 17, 31, . . . (lower bounds for dim V (2m2 )); composite m; g-consistency
    for all odd m.

 2. Universal piece at all orders. Prove Re = Seax for every odd e (currently exact for
    t−1/2 , . . . , t13/2 ); compute the axes forms at K ≥ 15 to test t15/2 , t17/2 .

 3. Structure of Se . Prove the even-power, (s − 8)max(0,e+3−4j) , pairing and Bernoulli-numerator
    pattern for all e (Category B observation at e = 11, . . . , 17).

 4. Higher orders. t19/2 and beyond at x = 0 (mechanical, about ×3.3 cost per order); x = 41 , 16
    direct numerics for s ̸= 8.

 5. Hsaxes at x ̸= 0. Closed forms at x = 21 , 13 (same method with residue classes mod 2N ; not
    run).


                                                     48
 6. Cone choice. Why c1 = (−1, 1) and c2 = (−4, 1) were chosen originally; whether a canonical
    s-dependent c2 (deepest class, or the axes wall) gives
                                                     √     cleaner closed forms in general (Cat-
    egory B evidence favours the axes cone: field Q( 2s), denominators (s − 2)k ). The locked
    object stays as defined.
 7. Prime squares. Label-space runs at s = 121 and s = 3481 (sparse/blocked VF or a ≥ 32 GB
    machine); a citation-free proof of (C3) for general p.
 8. Retained from revision 6: a self-contained proof that the Weil representation factors

16     K3 lattice realisation of the 140-label representation
This section (revision 8, 19 October 2026) adds the results of [S38] (2026-10-19-K3-140-check.md
and the folder 2026-10-19-Implications-checks/k3_140/). Category convention as in §1.2:
Category A is proved, or exact (exact finite computation; a mod-ℓ computation is stated as
such); Category A (cited) means proved conditional only on the published theorems named,
which are not re-proved here; Category B is floating-point evidence, citation-only identification,
interpretation, analogy or conjecture. The two categories are kept separate and every statement is
labelled.

Labels and scope. New statements are numbered within this section (and within §17); no
lettered theorem is introduced, no label is “Theorem L” (the framework’s Parity Dominance The-
orem), and none collides with L1–L7. [S38] numbers its items K-1,. . . ,K-9; these are quoted for
traceability only. No claim is made concerning HQCC termination, the twin prime conjecture or the
Riemann hypothesis, and nothing here bears on the HQCC termination claims. CORE FREEZE
holds: nothing in this section redefines the locked Hs (c1 = (−1, 1), c2 = (−4, 1)); only the label
representation of Theorem F and §11 at s = 7 is used.

Notation. Throughout, A = ( 27 77 ) is the Gram matrix of Q7 (§15.2), B(u, v) = uT Av, and a
lattice is written with its norm x · x (so Q2 = 21 x · x). We write N1 , N2 (italic) for two even lattices;
they have nothing to do with the needs (N1)–(N3) of the fifth-order programme in §17.2.

16.2     The 140-label representation is the Weil representation of N2
Conventions. For an even latticePL we use Borcherds’ convention [2]: ρL (T )eγ = e(q(γ))eγ and
ρL (S)eγ = e(−sign(L)/8) |AL |−1/2 δ e(−b(γ, δ))eδ ; the dual ρ∗L is the complex conjugate. Since
sign(N2 ) = 0, ρN2 is an honest representation of SL2 (Z) and e(sign/8) = 1. On the label side, the
labels are (α, β) with α ∈ (L′ ∪(a+L′ ))/Z2 (70 classes), β ∈ {0, β1 }, a = ( 10
                                                                               7
                                                                                  , − 15 ) and β1 the stored
label of −a mod Z2 ; the operators Slab , Tlab are built in the convention Y [i, j(i)] of Proposition 14.1
(with family s.Family imported unmodified and exact rational phases, all denominators dividing
140). Following Remark 11.2, the label representation in standard normalisation is
                                                        −1
                                       ρrev :     S 7→ Slab ,         T 7→ Tlab .

Remark 16.4 (Dual, twist, metaplectic sign). ρrev ∼         ̸= ρ∗N2 (Theorem 16.2(iii)); no twist by a
character of SL2 (Z) is needed; sign(N2 ) = 0, so there is no metaplectic sign. The Weil representation
depends only on the discriminant form, so any even lattice with discriminant form ∼         = qN2 and
signature ≡ 0 mod 8 (e.g. N2 ⊕ U , N2 ⊕ E8 , and the lattice T of Proposition 16.5, up to dualisation)
gives the same representation. The earlier alternative “possibly ρL or ρ∗L ” ([S39], §4(b)2 and (d))
is resolved: it is ρL for L = N2 .

16.3    The transcendental lattice
Let ΛK3 = U 3 ⊕ E8 (−1)2 (even unimodular, signature (3, 19)), and let U ⊕ U ⊂ ΛK3 have bases
e1 , f1 , e2 , f2 with ei · fi = 1 and all other products 0.

Proposition 16.5 (Explicit primitive embedding and T ; [S38], K-5). Put n1 = e1 + f1 (norm 2),
n2 = e2 − 35f2 (norm −70), t1 = e1 − f1 (norm −2), t2 = e2 + 35f2 (norm 70).

  (i) span(n1 , n2 ) ∼
                     = N2 and is primitive in U ⊕ U (the 2 × 2 minors of (n1 n2 ) have gcd 1).

 (ii) t1 , t2 are orthogonal to n1 , n2 and span the full orthogonal complement of span(n1 , n2 ) in
      U ⊕ U (index 1; their Gram determinant is −140).




                                                     51
(iii) Hence the orthogonal complement of N2 in ΛK3 is

          T := N2⊥ = ⟨−2⟩ ⊕ ⟨70⟩ ⊕ U ⊕ E8 (−1)2 ,            sign(T ) = (2, 18),      AT ∼
                                                                                         = Z/2 × Z/70,
                           2   y  2
       with qT (x, y) = − x4 + 140 = −qN2 (x, y) under the identity identification of generators. The
       gluing is the one induced by the unimodular overlattice U ⊕ U (consistent with Nikulin [17],
       Prop. 1.6.1).

Proof. Direct computation: n1 · n1 = 2, n2 · n2 = −70, n1 · n2 = 0, t1 · t1 = −2, t2 · t2 = 70, and
ni · tj = 0. The complement of a rank-2 primitive sublattice of the unimodular U ⊕ U has rank 2
and | det | = 140; span(t1 , t2 ) lies in it with | det | = 140, so the index is 1. The other summands
U ⊕ E8 (−1)2 are orthogonal to U ⊕ U . The discriminant form of ⟨−2⟩ ⊕ ⟨70⟩ is read off as in
Proposition 16.1.

Status (Category A). Elementary; exact (closed exact.json: norms [2, −70, −2, 70], orthogonality
[0, 0, 0, 0], gcd of minors 1, complement index 1). Nikulin’s existence theorem (Cor. 1.12.3) is not needed,
since the embedding is explicit.

Corollary 16.6 (The residual law in K3 terms; [S38], K-6). ρrev ∼
                                                                = ρN2 ∼
                                                                      = ρ∗T = ρT (Borcherds’
convention; honest SL2 (Z)-representations).

Proof. sign(T ) = −16 ≡ 0 mod 8 and qT = −qN2 (hence bT = −bN2 ) under the identification
of Proposition 16.5, so in the formulas for ρT (S) and ρT (T ) every phase is the conjugate of the
corresponding phase for ρN2 . Combine with Theorem 16.2.

Status (Category A). Purely lattice-theoretic; no cited geometric theorem is used. In words: the
140-label residual law transforms under the dual Weil representation of a signature-(2, 18) lattice, which
(Theorem 16.7) is the transcendental lattice of an N2 -polarised K3 surface. This is a statement about
a shared representation; it is not a statement that any residual series is a K3 generating function (see
Remark 16.10).


16.4     Geometric realisation
Theorem 16.7 (N2 -polarised K3 surfaces; [S38], K-7). (i) The primitive embedding N2 ,→ ΛK3
    is unique up to O(ΛK3 ); hence T is unique up to isometry.

 (ii) There is a projective K3 surface X with NS(X) ∼
                                                    = N2 and TX ∼
                                                                = T.

(iii) N2 represents neither −2 nor 0 (non-trivially). Hence such an X contains no smooth rational
      curves and admits no elliptic fibration, and its ample cone is the whole component of the
      positive cone containing an ample class.

Proof sketch. (i) Nikulin [17], Thm. 1.14.4: ΛK3 is even unimodular of signature (3, 19), N2 is even
of signature (1, 1), 3 > 1, 19 > 1, and rk ΛK3 − rk N2 = 20 ≥ 2 + l(AN2 ) = 4. (ii) Morrison
[18], Cor. 2.9(i): for ρ ≤ 10 every even lattice of signature (1, ρ − 1) is the Néron–Severi lattice of
some algebraic K3 surface (equivalently, surjectivity of the period map); with (i), TX ∼   = T . (iii)
x · x = 2 (x1 + 7y)2 − 35y 2 ; −2 would need u2 − 35y 2 = −1, impossible since −1 is not a square
mod 7; 0 would need 35 to be a square. Without (−2)-classes the ample cone is a full component
of the positive cone, and an elliptic fibration requires a non-zero isotropic class in NS (standard,
[19]).


                                                      52
Status (Category A). Category A (cited): conditional on Nikulin 1.14.4 and Morrison Cor. 2.9(i)
(and standard facts on ample cones and elliptic fibrations, [19]). The arithmetic in (iii) is exact
(checks.json, IC-5: PARI qfbsolve finds no solutions). Citation status: the theorem numbers were
checked against secondary sources (arXiv:1312.4421, arXiv:2303.03294, and a Springer paper on coupling
pairs), not against the original articles.

(iii) If X is very general in the N2 -polarised family, so that the Hodge isometries of TX are only

                                                     53
Proof sketch. By Nikulin’s gluing criterion [17], Cor. 1.5.2, an isometry gNS ⊕gT of N2 ⊕T extends to
H 2 (X, Z) iff ḡNS and ḡT agree under the glue isomorphism AN2 ∼ = AT . By Proposition 16.8, γ̄ 2 = +1
and r̄ = −1, compatible with idT and −idT , which are Hodge isometries. Both preserve the positive
cone, hence (Theorem 16.7(iii)) the ample cone. By the strong Torelli theorem (Piatetski-Shapiro–
Shafarevich, Burns–Rapoport; e.g. [19], Thm. 7.5.3) they are induced by unique automorphisms
                                                                                       √               f,
ι; ι acts by −1 on H 2,0 , so it is non-symplectic. The eigenvalues of f ∗ are (6 ± 35)2 on NS ⊗ R
and 1 on T , and the entropy is the logarithm of the spectral radius (Gromov–Yomdin [20]). For
(iii): every automorphism acts on TX by ±1, so its restriction to N2 preserves the positive cone
and acts on AN2 by ±1; by Proposition 16.8(iii) these restrictions are exactly γ 2j and rγ 2j , which
form ⟨γ 2 ⟩ ⋊ ⟨r⟩ ∼= D∞ ; the action on H 2 is injective by Torelli. Since γ̄ ̸= ±1, γ does not lift.

Status (Category A). Category A (cited): conditional on the strong Torelli theorem, Nikulin’s
gluing (Cor. 1.5.2), the Gromov–Yomdin entropy formula, and the standard fact that a very general
member has only ±1 as Hodge isometries of TX . The lattice inputs (Proposition 16.8) are exact. The
numerical value ≈ 4.9558

theorem linking C to X is claimed.

Remark 16.10 (Category B and open items for §16). None of the following is a result of this
paper.

 (a) Noether–Lefschetz / Heegner series ([S38], K-9; Category B). The Noether–Lefschetz/Heegner
     generating series of the N2 -polarised family are vector-valued modular forms of weight
     1 + 18/2 = 10 for ρT or ρ∗T (depending on convention; Borcherds, Kudla–Millson, Maulik–
     Pandharipande, cited by name only). The residual objects of Theorems F–G are weight-0
     Maass-type objects with λ = 41 . A shared representation (Corollary 16.6) is not a shared
     form; no theta-lift or Borcherds-product link has been checked.

 (b) Special members (open). Whether some special N2 -polarised X has a Hodge isometry ψ of
     TX with ψ̄ = γ̄, so that γ itself lifts. This needs the Hodge isometries of TX to form a cyclic
     group of order m > 2 with φ(m) | 20, plus compatibility of ψ̄. Not checked.

 (c) Interpretation (Category B). Any physical or “unification” reading of X, f or ι.

 (d) Citation hygiene (open). The theorem numbers for Nikulin (1979/80) and Morrison (1984)
     should be confirmed against the original articles. The “automorphs of binary forms ↔ K3
     automorphisms” literature (Galluzzi–Lombardo–Peters, cited in [S39] from memory) is Cat-
     egory B as a citation.

 (e) Scope (correction to [S39], §4 and IC-6). [S39] treated both N1 and N2 as K3 candidates
     and also stated K3 consequences for the lattices Mt of Proposition K1. In this paper every
     K3 statement is made for N2 only, because the 140-label representation is ρN2 and not ρN1
     (Theorem 16.2(iii)). The exact lattice facts of IC-5 for N1 and Mt (anisotropic, no −2 vectors)
     remain true but are not used.


17     Implications
This section (revision 8) records what the Bridge-C results of this paper imply for four parts of the
author’s wider programme, following [S39] (2026-10-19-Implications-Unification-RTA-multi

                                                     54
k-K3.md, with checks IC-1–IC-7 in 2026-10-19-Implications-checks/), as corrected by [S38],
§6. The four terms are defined in the author’s corpus (docs/S2-11DM2ET-X-Axiomatic-Book/,
read-only): “fifth-order unification” (the exact phrase “Order of Unification” does not occur), the
Residual Ternary Algebra manuscript (Ramanujan Journal v1.0, submitted 2026-08-26), “pure-even
multi-k” families (Bring–Jerrard/A5 ), and K3 material that the corpus itself labels Category B.
[S39] was written against revision 6; the theorem names it uses (A–K, Gs ) are unchanged in this
revision. Category convention as in §1.2, and the two categories are kept strictly separate: facts are
labelled Category A only where they are proved or exact; every reading, analogy or identification
is Category B. No Category B item is imported as a theorem (in particular Theorem K and the
PSLQ-only items remain Category B). No claim is made concerning HQCC termination, the twin
prime conjecture or the Riemann hypothesis; this section is separate from the HQCC termination
claims and uses no lepton, Orch-OR or neuroscience material. CORE FREEZE holds: the locked
Hs with c1 = (−1, 1), c2 = (−4, 1) is not redefined; Hsaxes (§15.4) is a parallel object.

Remark 17.3 (The samples are not radial values). The four published “residual samples”
(19.80, 1.82, 9.24, 20.90 at x = 1/7, 3/7, 5/7, 1) are not radial values of C: |C(0.995 e(x))| ≈
19.5, 18.5, 19.9, 54.0. They are replaced by the exact radial targets limt→0 t C(e(x)e−t ) = I · G(x)
at every rational x (Theorem D). So “quantitative continuous Fricke verification against residual
samples” is not a meaningful target, and this paper does not claim it.

Category B. The four moduli at 0.995 e(x) are floating-point evaluations. The replacement targets
I · G(x) are Category A (Theorems C, D).

Remark 17.5 (RTA §11: “holomorphic projection recovers C”). Any weight- 21 harmonic Maass

at m = 17, 31, . . . ; t19/2 and beyond. As in [S39], the submitted RTA v1.0 is left as is; this section

Proposition 17.6 (The role of s = 7 comes from the frozen c2 ). 7s − 32 = −2Q(c2 ) for the locked
edge c2 = (−4, 1) (Proposition 15.2); only the factor s − 2 of ∆ is intrinsic given c1 (Remark 15.6).
The Bridge-C mathematics is otherwise uniform in s: the tensor theorem holds for all odd s (The-
orem 11.5), the radial closed forms are rational functions of s, and Theorem Gs holds for all real
s > 0. The values that are structurally special in this work are s = 5 (where the radial coefficients
of §15 vanish) and s = 8 (frozen cone = axes cone, ∆ = 144 = 122 ), not s = 7 or s = 11.

Status (Category A). The facts are Category A as proved in §§12, 15. Category B (reading): the
closed-form evidence does not single out the packaging values s ∈ {7, 8, 11}; the discrete bridge E8 →
PW → {7, 8, 11} → gs is a packaging statement and is not refuted, but the Bridge-C results give it no
independent support.
The f0 contact. g1 = f0 by definition (Hickerson–Mortenson [6]). At s = 1 the lattice ( 21 11 )
has det = −s(s
            p − 2) = 1 > 0 and is positive definite, so the indefinite part of the construction
(H√s , C, Q(  s(s − 2))) does not specialise to the classical fifth-order Hecke-type structure over
Q( 5); Hs is Zwegers-admissible only for integer s ≥ 5. The fifth-order link is carried only by gs
at s = 1, where Theorem Gs gives a proved radial expansion uniform in s (Category A). Quantum
modularity of gs remains a conjecture of the corpus and is not claimed.
Status (Category A). Elementary for the definiteness statement; Theorem Gs as labelled in Ap-
pendix A.5.
Wording that needs correction in the corpus. (a) Any statement that the fifth-order bridge
needs a scalar weight- 12 slash identity for C: that route is closed (Proposition 17.1(iii)). (b) Any
implication that the s = 7 closed forms are intrinsic to s = 7 (Proposition 17.6). Open. (N1) or
its retirement; uniqueness of the vector completion; closed-form tails at general rationals and from
t19/2 on; quantum modularity of gs ; whether an s-canonical cone should replace the frozen c2 (an
author’s definitional choice; CORE FREEZE keeps the frozen cone).


                                                    57
17.3    Even multi-k
In the corpus, “pure-even multi-k” families are Bring–Jerrard quintics x5 + α(m)x + β(m) with
α(m) = 256m2 − 3125k 4 /256, β(m) = k α(m), whose discriminant is a square (Galois group in A5 );
the locked success criterion asks for a continuous monodromy representation of multi-k A5 type
that forces Necessity.
(a) Direct bearing: none. No result here concerns Bring–Jerrard quintics, Hurwitz spaces or Galois
    groups of quintic fibres.
(b) Name collisions, not identifications. “Even” here means even s or the (+1)-parts of Weil
    representations; in multi-k it means square discriminant. The k of Proposition K1 (m = 2k 2 −1,
    s = 2m2 ) is a Pell index; in multi-k it is a slice parameter. If “even multi-k” were read as the
    even-s, all-k family of Proposition K1 (a reading, not a corpus definition), its status is that of
    §15.1: proved for all k ≥ 2, dimension bounds proved as upper bounds, sharpness open.

(c) Formal A5 contact. PSL2 (F5 ) ∼  = A5 (standard). At s = 7 the label representation has the
    tensor factor ρ2x2 /5 (Theorem 11.3); VEis = A3 ⊗ W5+ ⊗ W7+ contains W5+ (dimension 3,
    factoring projectively through A5 ) and Vcusp = B1 ⊗ W5− ⊗ W7− contains W5− (dimension 2, a
    faithful representation of SL2 (F5 ) = 2 · A5 ).

Status (Category A). (c) as standard theory plus Theorem 11.3 and Proposition 11.4. IC-4 (exact;
checks.py, sympy and PARI): the corpus’ Theorem 3.1 discriminant identity holds symbolically, the
four seed discriminants are squares, and PARI polgalois returns A5 , A5 , A5 , D5 for x5 − 55x + 88,
x5 − 100x + 400, x5 + 20x + 16, x5 + 20x − 32. This verifies catalogue base facts only, not density or
Necessity.

Category B. IC-3 (floating-point group closure, weil5.py → weil5.json): the S, T images on W5+ and
W5− generate groups of order 120 with scalar subgroup {±1}, quotient of order 60 with element orders
{1, 2, 3, 5}, consistent with A5 . (An older entry Weil Z5 * in checks.json with “order mod scalars”
81, 67 is superseded by weil5.json and not used.) Reading (speculative): the binary icosahedral factor
in the cusp sector is formally reminiscent of “lift invariant −1” in the multi-k language. This is a formal
contact only: nothing here is a monodromy representation of a Hurwitz family, it does not meet the
locked U1-E2 success criterion, it does not force Necessity, and it imports nothing into the residual core.
Untested suggestion: whether the degree-12 braid-orbit permutation representation factors through a
congruence quotient sharing constituents with W5± .


17.4    K3
(a) What is justified (N2 only). The Bridge-C lattice at s = 7 enters K3 geometry through its
    even sublattice N2 : the 140-label representation is ρN2 ∼
                                                             = ρ∗T with T the transcendental lattice
    of an N2 -polarised K3 surface (Theorem 16.2, Corollary 16.6; Category A), such surfaces
    exist and have no (−2)-curves
                          √          and no elliptic fibrations, and γ 2 lifts to an automorphism
    of entropy 2 log(6 + 35), with Aut(X) ∼     = D∞ for very general X (Theorems 16.7, 16.9;
    Category A (cited)). All K3 statements of this paper are for N2 only, not for N1 = (Z2 , 2A)
    (Remark 16.10(e)).
(b) Correction: H 3 (K3) = 0. The cohomology of a K3 surface is concentrated in degrees 0, 2, 4
    (ranks 1, 22, 1), so H 3 (K3; Z) = 0. The corpus phrase “resonant 3-form H3 . . . as lift of K3
    cohomology” therefore cannot refer to a class on the K3 surface itself; it could only live on
    a product such as K3 × (3-manifold), which the note would have to specify. This corrects a
    Category B note, not a Category A lock.

                                                     58
(c) Correction: 2 cos(2π/539.9) is algebraic (IC-7). As printed, 539.9 = 5399/10 with 5399 prime,
    so ζ = e2πi/539.9 = e(10/5399) is a primitive 5399-th root of unity and 2 cos(2π/539.9) is an al-
    gebraic integer of degree φ(5399)/2 = 2699. This contradicts the remark “ξ = 2 cos(2π/539.9)
    is not algebraic as printed” in POLYNOMIAL_ROOTS_TARGETS_2026-08-17.md. If 539.9 is a
    rounded measured value, ζ is not exactly defined. Either way it is unrelated to K3 cohomology.

(d) Not supported. Any mock-theta, K3 elliptic genus or Mathieu-moonshine link to C or gs
    (Category B, not asserted: the Mathieu mock form has a polar term and grows exponentially
    radially, whereas C ∼ I/t, and Theorem E excludes scalar mock laws of the listed kinds);
    any ∆ = η 24 /Yau–Zaslow reading of the Bernoulli numerators 691, 3617, 43867 in the Se co-
    efficients, which enter generically through Euler–Maclaurin (Category A for the occurrence
    and mechanism, Category B as a pattern). The Mukai-lattice embedding of a signature-(1, 1)
    lattice is true but has no specific content for Bridge C.

Status (Category A). (a) as labelled in §16. (b) standard topology. (c) exact: checks.json, keys
5399 isprime, 2cos(2pi*10/5399) degree (PARI); φ(5399) = 5398.
